# IYAKKI feature matrix

The single place to see what exists, what is half-done, what is next and what is on its way out.
It is written for people **and** for code assistants: one row per feature, a stable ID, the phase it belongs to,
how far along it is, and the unit tests that prove it. `crates/iyakki-core/tests/feature_matrix.rs` checks this file,
so a row cannot claim a test that does not exist or a status that contradicts its progress.

**Totals:** <!-- summary:start -->done 286 · partial 204 · planned 59 · blocked 3 · deprecated 3 · total 555<!-- summary:end -->

## How to use it

| You want to… | Do this |
|---|---|
| **Prioritise** | Take `planned` and `partial` rows, sort by `Pri` (P0 first), then by `Progress` (finish the nearly-done first). `blocked` rows say why in Notes. |
| **Start a feature** | Set Status `partial`, Progress above 0, and add the tests you write to the `Tests` column in the same change. |
| **Finish a feature** | Status `done`, Progress `100%`, and at least one test (or a `script:` check for things only a VM can prove). |
| **Deprecate** | Set Status `deprecated` and write what replaces it in Notes (`Replaced by F-…`). Leave the row in place until the code is removed; then delete the row in the same change. |
| **Add a feature** | New row with the next free number in its area. IDs never change and are never reused. |
| **Check your edit** | `cargo test -p iyakki-core --test feature_matrix` (also runs in `cargo test --workspace`). |

**Columns.** *ID* `F-<AREA>-<nn>`. *Phase*: P0 cross-cutting · P1 core + WASM · P2 snapshots and memory · P3 hardware and editor model · P4 packaging, network, swarm · P5 shell and permissions · P6 AI services (models, RAG, vault, MCP) · P7 assistant (chat sessions, skills, agent, voice) · P8 code editor · P9 persistence · P10 web and cloud. *Status*: `done` · `partial` · `planned` · `blocked` · `deprecated`. *Pri*: P0 critical … P3 nice to have. *Tests*: `crate::path::test_name` (the crate directory is `crates/<crate>`); `script:` entries are files that perform a check outside `cargo test` (QEMU boots, audits) and must exist.

| F-HW-03 | USB camera quirks, automatic: an in-house table of known UVC cameras (vendor:product -> `uvcvideo` quirks value, optionally microphone off), starting with `1d6c:0103` (Fullhan HD 4MP webcam) -> `0x100`, microphone off as a precaution; at the start of boot `uvcvideo` and `snd-usb-audio` are blacklisted for alias loading (`/etc/modprobe.d/iyakki-camera.conf`) and IYAKKI loads them by name after a known camera's microphone and video interfaces are held back (`authorized=0`), so the first probe already has the quirk; a camera that stopped answering (-110) is reported at once, not retried; at boot and on every USB `add` uevent the video control interface is (re)bound with the quirk and the global `uvcvideo` quirks parameter is put back at once; any other UVC camera with no video node 4 s after it appeared and a `UVC probe control` failure in the kernel log is retried once with `0x100`, then `0x2`; plain log lines (`camera: 1d6c:0103 needed uvcvideo quirk 0x100`) | P5 | On a Mac mini 2018 the hot-plug path (stock -32, then rebind with 0x100 within 11 ms) worked and the camera recorded video; the boot path (camera plugged in at power-on) failed in the first version and was reworked, unverified on hardware. | NOT tried on a real adapter: the logic, the order of steps, failure and restore are tested against a fake driver and a fake `/sys`; whether a given Ethernet or Wi-Fi driver accepts a new address with the link down (tg3 and most do; some refuse and are reported as "not supported by this driver"), and whether the supplicant's scan randomisation works on a given radio, are unknown until tried. Only the address at start-up and at join is controlled: if the supplicant roams to another saved network by itself the address stays until the next join or `iyakki net mac apply`. Per-interface and per-network overrides are `net.conf` and CLI only (the page chooses per kind). |

with made-up `/proc/asound` trees and `amixer` output in its documented shape; this build PC has no sound card. The T2 card's shape (`AppleT2x1 - Apple T2 Audio`, `pcm0p` Speaker, `pcm2p` Codec Output, `pcm3c`, one control `Codec Output Jack`) was read on the Mac mini 2018 with apple-bce loaded by hand, and `aplay -D plughw:1,0` played there; the selector code itself has not run on the Mac yet (needs a rebuilt image). Engines that speak by themselves (espeak-ng or speech-dispatcher speaking directly) still use ALSA's default device. Without `amixer`, which HDMI device a monitor is on is inferred from the ELD files (first connected monitor on the first HDMI device on Intel). Nothing under `/etc` is written. |
| F-SND-03 | Software volume for outputs without a hardware volume: IYAKKI writes `/etc/asound.conf` (marker first line; a file without it is never changed; nothing while a sound server runs) with an ALSA `softvol` device `iyakkivol` over the chosen output (control `IYAKKI` on its card, -51..0 dB) made `default` and `iyakki` over a `dmix` of the device (key `24680 + card*64 + device`, 48 kHz; 16-bit stereo, the T2 chip's 24-bit with its speaker as one channel through a `route`) so IYAKKI's players and the web engine play at once, falling back to the exclusive `plughw` where the dmix cannot be opened, or a plain plug when the output's card has a hardware `Master`/`PCM`/`Speaker`/`Headphone` volume (HDMI and S/PDIF never count); rewritten whenever the chosen output changes (monitor, headphones, USB), with players restarted; the softvol is primed (10 ms of silence) so its control exists, then `amixer -c C sset IYAKKI N%` sets the volume of Settings -> Sound; mute is 0 % with the level kept (IYAKKI's players also play silence); IYAKKI's players use `-D iyakki` and stop scaling their samples once the control holds the level, other programs (ffplay) follow `default`; each output's last volume is kept in the settings file and restored; Settings -> Sound, the card row and the Devices popover's Mute move IYAKKI's volume for that card; `iyakki-devctl` finds the `IYAKKI` control and mutes switchless controls by 0 % with the level remembered | P5 | partial | P1 | 80% | The vendors' download addresses and version sources are the ones documented today and may change. AWS CLI, Google Cloud CLI and espflash publish no checksum, so they are only protected by HTTPS from the vendor's host; the chip tools (esptool, espflash) need no network permission to run but need access to the serial device, and the Arduino CLI downloads its board packages itself; the Azure CLI and esptool are installed from PyPI with pip (needs python3 with venv); not tried with a real board. Only Linux x86-64 and ARM64. Installed tools are updated by pressing Update (no automatic updates). |
| F-CLI-03 | Container cluster on the machine itself: from the k3s entry an administrator starts and stops k3s (a background process of IYAKKI, kubeconfig in IYAKKI's own folder, clean SIGTERM stop) when this is a real machine (root, cgroup v2, k3s installed; otherwise it says exactly what is missing); the chat can check it (`cluster_status`), start and stop it (`cluster_start`, `cluster_stop`, with approval) and run code to practise or test with `sandbox_run` (Python, Node or shell) in a fresh Job with an unprivileged user, no capabilities, no cluster token, memory, processor, disk and time limits, no network unless the person allows it for that run, output returned and the job removed; `kubectl` through `run_command` is pointed at this cluster | P7 | partial | P2 | 60% | `iyakki-agent::cluster::tests::the_cluster_says_what_is_missing_and_only_starts_on_a_real_machine_with_k3s`<br>`iyakki-agent::cluster::tests::the_cluster_starts_becomes_ready_runs_code_in_a_limited_job_and_stops_cleanly`<br>`iyakki-agent::cluster::tests::a_failed_job_a_network_run_and_a_k3s_that_dies_are_reported`<br>`iyakki-agent::agent::tests::the_chat_starts_the_cluster_and_runs_python_in_a_limited_container_for_an_administrator_only`<br>`iyakki-shell::apps::cli::tests::the_k3s_entry_starts_and_stops_the_container_cluster_after_confirming_and_says_what_is_missing` | Tried with a real k3s 1.37 (2026-10-11): in a privileged container and inside the booted x86 image under QEMU, python and shell sandboxes run as uid 65534, the system folders are read-only, a 1 GB allocation is stopped and reported in words, an endless loop is stopped at its deadline, a failing program reports its exit status. On the image, k3s runs in a private mount namespace whose root is a tmpfs, because the RAM root cannot be left with pivot_root, and the Job asks for no disk-space limit there. Network-policy enforcement (no network unless allowed) is enforced on the image too (the kernel needed the bridge and physdev options; checked: no-network code gets ConnectionRefused). Not tried on a physical machine or on aarch64. k3s needs about 1 GB of memory, and the images (python, node, alpine) come from docker.io the first time. Stopping k3s leaves its containers to its own shutdown. No persistent storage or services are set up; code is limited to 64 KB, one script per run. |

| F-CLI-06 | SSH safety for the assistant, the terminal and the tab: hosts follow the account's network rules; the assistant uses `run_command` (`ssh`, `scp`, `sftp`) which always asks, even in Auto; host keys go in a per-person 0600 `known_hosts`, an unknown host is refused without starting the client and only the person can trust it after seeing the `SHA256:` fingerprint, a changed key is a hard refusal; private keys come only from the vault as `-i uvault:ssh.<name>` into a 0600 file in a 0700 folder that is removed after the run (passphrases from the vault through an askpass helper, never an argument); Also done (layout of real pages): `display:inline-block` as an atomic box in the line (shrink-to-fit width unless `width` is given, its own blocks inside, margin, padding, border, `height`/`min-height`, background and radius honoured, `vertical-align` top, middle or bottom, wrapping to the next line when it does not fit, centred or right-aligned lines of them), plain inline elements with horizontal padding, margin and border (room beside the words, background over the padding), `display:inline` on block tags, form controls sized by style sheets (`width`, `height`, margin, `vertical-align`) and by `size`, pictures and controls centred by `<center>`, `align=center` and `text-align`, `<br clear=all>`, absolutely positioned inline elements as boxes (and out of grid and flex rows), real tables (cell contents are blocks so tables nest; column widths from content, `width` in pixels or percent, `colspan`/`rowspan`, `cellpadding`/`cellspacing`, `align`, `valign`, `nowrap`, `bgcolor`, centred by `<center>`; quirks mode (no doctype) does not pass `text-align` into tables), presentational markup (`<center>`, `<font color size>`, `<u>`, `<s>`, `<small>`, `<big>`, `<mark>`, `align`, `bgcolor`, `width`), grid areas (`grid-template-areas` with `grid-area` and fixed or `fr` column tracks), duplicate linked style sheets counted once and up to 40 sheets per page. `::first-line`/`::first-letter`/`::marker`, an empty generated box with only a background, percentages in padding and margins; CSS animations and transitions (static end state), `@font-face` web fonts, `clip-path`, inset shadows, multiple shadows or backgrounds (only the first counts), percentage or `px` gradient hints, true radial gradients (filled with the average colour), `position: fixed` and `sticky` (they stay where the flow puts them), percentage offsets, `z-index` stacking contexts (a positioned box with a z-index is just drawn in z order over or under the flow), floats inside flex or grid items and tables, a float's `height` (no box height from CSS), `opacity` as a true group (each colour is blended toward the page behind); JavaScript: closed shadow roots, shadow-scoped styles, | F-BROW-43 | Embedded full web engine: F (or the Full browser button) shows the tab's page with WebKit inside the browser's page area, in the desktop window, on the framebuffer and in the web front end alike. A helper program per tab renders offscreen and sends pixels through a shared file (two slots, dirty rectangles, acknowledgements); the shell draws them 1:1 (HiDPI by the interface scale) and sends mouse, wheel, keys (X11 keysyms), pastes and zoom. The address bar, back, forward, reload, tabs, zoom, stars, history and downloads stay the browser's own; the account's host list is applied to the first address and to every address the engine reports, downloads use the browser's download code, a crashed or missing helper falls back to the built-in reader with a clear message, Esc hands the keyboard back and then returns to the reader. Window mode runs `dist/webview/host-helper.py` (WebKitGTK, a private Xvfb for video): measured here, Google search and YouTube (search by typing, clicking a result, a video playing at about 20 frames a second on a busy machine) render inside the browser. The image's helper is `dist/webview/wpe-helper` (WPE WebKit 2.50 through libwpe and WPEBackend-fdo shared-memory buffers, Mesa llvmpipe): measured in QEMU (4 CPUs, KVM), google.com, Wikipedia and YouTube render inside the browser, typing, wheel and clicks reach the page, a YouTube video (VP9 + Opus) plays at about 28 pictures a second, helper 91 MB, web process 0.3-1.1 GB. Clicks no longer miss above 100% interface scale (the helper divided positions by the scale twice; found at 3840x2160 in QEMU); `navigator.clipboard.writeText`, copy and cut events reach the shell's clipboard; `BrowserApp::open_in_new_tab(url, cx)` opens an address in a new tab (engine when offered). Video route: an installed player, else the embedded engine (the `web` image), else the message `Video needs the web image or an installed player (mpv, VLC, ffplay)`. The engine only sees `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/<token>/` (`mediaserve.rs`, design in docs/MEDIA-SEARCH.md). Unit tests use the pure-Rust fake helper. Also run for real in QEMU (KVM, the `web` image, `dist/buildroot/qemu-media-test.py`, 2026-10-13): the samples appear in a fresh library, the MP3 plays (the virtual sound card received about 1.6 MB of audio), the sample video plays in the MEDIA page through WPE WebKit (moving picture, time and seek bar, Space pauses) and two addresses from a local server download into Music and Videos (the folders were made on the way). Not verified: sound of the video (the guest mixer starts muted), a real speaker, a real GPU, bare metal, H.264. A plain video is buffered in `/var/tmp` (RAM on the live image), which the image lacked until this work (docs/FULL-BROWSER.md). |

Not verified on real hardware: streaming, controls, the frame rate, a 4 MP camera's decode speed (the preview is shrunk to 1280 wide; photos keep the full frame). Saving checks the account's write scope (`/home/<user>/Pictures`). Files and folders saved in a person's Pictures/Videos are given to that account (passwd entry, else the home folder's owner; never root) and file names use the time zone of the top-bar clock; the ownership change itself needs root and is tested with a fake chown only. |

| F-NETSEC-01 | New-device alerts: IYAKKI only listens: the system's neighbour table (`/proc/net/arp`) is read every half minute (and a NET SCAN's findings are added); the first look is taken as what is normally here, after that a hardware address never seen before is a new device, an administrator is told on screen and in the audit log, and NET SCAN → Devices lists everything seen (strangers first) where it can be trusted, trusted all, named, forgotten or the watch switched off | P7 | done | P2 | 100% | `iyakki-agent::devwatch::tests::the_neighbour_table_is_read_keeping_only_machines_that_answered`<br>`iyakki-agent::devwatch::tests::the_first_look_is_what_is_normal_and_later_strangers_are_new_until_trusted`<br>`iyakki-agent::services::tests::the_device_watch_reads_the_neighbour_table_in_the_background_and_reports_strangers_once`<br>`iyakki-shell::apps::scan::tests::the_devices_view_lists_strangers_first_and_an_administrator_trusts_names_and_forgets_them`<br>`iyakki-shell::shell::tests::an_administrator_is_told_about_a_new_device_on_the_network_and_an_ordinary_user_is_not` | The neighbour table only holds machines this computer has talked to, so a quiet device is noticed once something (a scan, a DHCP exchange, a ping) makes the two speak; this is not packet sniffing (that would need raw sockets). | F-UPD-01 | System update bundle (`.tgz`): a signed archive with the kernel, the initial RAM disk (and later the side image), `release.json` (version, variant, file checksums, minimum base version, release notes) and a detached signature checked against the release key in the image; downloadable from an update channel (portal URL, a private URL, or a file or USB stick for offline machines); staged on the data volume and applied at the next restart, which the person approves (never an automatic reboot) | P4 | planned | P1 | 0% | — | Post-beta (v1.x), decided 2026-10-20: the beta only updates apps from the store. Needs the larger boot partition (F-UPD-01) and `iyakki-usb --update`; tested in QEMU with a deliberately broken bundle. |
| F-UPD-03 | Small fix updates as an overlay: a second initial RAM disk with only the changed files (the iyakki program, the web helper, scripts) that GRUB loads after the base one, so a bug fix is tens of megabytes, not the whole 160 MB set; a major update (kernel, modules, base libraries) replaces the full set | P4 | planned | P2 | 0% | — | Post-beta (v1.x), decided 2026-10-20. Overlay must name the base it fits (`min_base`); several overlays fold into one on the next full update. |
